# Decision Log

> **2026-06-15 — DEC-184 logged — ORCH-1144 [universal experience-create chooser] CLOSED PASS (Seth iOS device-confirmed).** Two decisions. **(1) The two Gemini experience parsers (Ve5 restaurant-menu, Ve6 play-activities) are DECOUPLED from `brand.venueCategory`.** Previously `experiences.tsx` routed restaurant→Ve5, play→Ve6, every other category (creative/arts, null) → NEITHER, gated by `canGenerateExperiencesFromMenu`/`canGenerateExperiencesFromActivities`. Now every brand reaches BOTH unconditionally via explicit user pick (parseMode from `/experience/snap?mode=` URL param, never inferred from the brand); predicate files + category router DELETED. Rationale (Seth): "all users can benefit from the parsers regardless of their kind of brand." No money/trust gate weakened — server-side brand-ownership is the real gate (preserved); parser writes only `agent_pending_actions` drafts (publish/checkout gated downstream). Codified **I-PROPOSED-1144-PARSERS-CATEGORY-AGNOSTIC** (ACTIVE). **(2) The chooser is an in-place step INSIDE `UniversalCreatorSheet`, NOT a separate route/sheet.** First built as a `/experience/choose` route; Seth's device feedback ("it opens up in another sheet when it can open up in the same drop down for continuity") drove a refactor to an in-sheet step + Back affordance (omitted when opened directly at the experience step from the Hub-tab CTA via `initialStep="experience"`); `/experience/choose` + `ExperienceCreateChooser` DELETED. Trade-off: two-step internal sheet state within the `TopSheet heightMode="compact"` contract (DEC-152) vs. a stateless route — chosen for UX continuity. Also decommissioned dead `experience/coming-soon.tsx`. EXIT: permanent unless a future ORCH re-splits the chooser. Cross-refs: `specs/SPEC_ORCH-1144_*`, `investigations/INVESTIGATE_ORCH-1144_*`, `reports/{IMPLEMENT,TEST}_ORCH-1144*`, `design/ORCH-1144/COPY_*`. Merge PR #488 `01050d289`; prod OTA iOS `1202ac95` / Android `35573d3d`. Operator sign-off: "Chooser can stay 'Create An Experience'. Drive it till completion" + "everything looks good" (iOS device PASS).

> **2026-05-25 — DEC-180 logged — ORCH-0957 [Storage image transformation overage] CLOSED CONDITIONAL PASS Grade A.** Decision: adopt **Tier B** (pre-generate 384×384 JPEG thumbnails at place-photo ingest; rewrite collage helper to read non-metered object-endpoint thumbs) as the canonical fix for Supabase Storage Image Transformation cost control on the place-intelligence collage pipeline. Operator chose Tier B over three alternatives evaluated by Claude `mingla-forensics` INVESTIGATE phase: **Tier A** (drop the transform entirely) was eliminated because it re-triggers the `WORKER_RESOURCE_LIMIT 546` OOM that ORCH-0737 v6 explicitly solved at 2026-05-06 — native-resolution photo decode at parallel-12 outer prep would peak ~1.1 GB per worker vs 256 MB edge-function memory limit. **Tier C** (CDN cache layer in front of Supabase Storage) was eliminated by Supabase docs verification (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/manage-your-usage/storage-image-transformations): billing counts **distinct origin images per billing period regardless of request frequency**, so a CDN cache reduces nothing (each unique image already costs $0 on repeat fetches). **Tier D** (stop mirroring Google Places photos into the `place-photos` bucket; serve directly via Google CDN `=w192-h192` Pattern 2 at zero cost) was paired as a future option but **explicitly deferred per operator decision 2026-05-25 — Tier B is shipped standalone**; the existing 88,133 photo mirror is kept (durability hedge against Google `photo_reference` expiry per OQ-1; operator may revisit Tier D in a future ORCH if cost trajectory at scale demands further reduction). Pairing rationale: Tier B alone eliminates the bill structurally for both new ingests (born-backfilled) AND historical pool (one-time admin-driven backfill via new `backfill-place-photo-thumbs` edge fn at $0 cost using direct-fetch + in-memory imagescript resize — NEVER the metered transform endpoint). EXIT condition: permanent unless operator opens follow-up to also adopt Tier D. Cross-references: investigation `Mingla_Artifacts/investigations/INVESTIGATE_ORCH-0957_STORAGE_IMAGE_TRANSFORM_OVERAGE.md` §6 (Fix Strategy with full 4-tier comparison + cost projections at 1× / 10× / 100× growth); SPEC `Mingla_Artifacts/specs/SPEC_ORCH-0957_STORAGE_IMAGE_TRANSFORM_OVERAGE.md` §2 (Scope and non-goals); implementation `reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0957_STORAGE_IMAGE_TRANSFORM_OVERAGE.md`; deploy `reports/DEPLOY_ORCH-0957_STORAGE_IMAGE_TRANSFORM_OVERAGE.md`; QA `reports/QA_ORCH-0957_STORAGE_IMAGE_TRANSFORM_OVERAGE_REPORT.md` CONDITIONAL PASS P0:0 P1:0 P2:0 P3:2 P4:4. New invariants `I-NO-METERED-READ-ON-INGESTED-PHOTOS` (ACTIVE) + `I-EXTERNAL-API-DOCS-VERIFIED` (broadened DRAFT → ACTIVE per Discovery D-1 to add metering/cost rules alongside enums/payloads). New CI gate `.github/scripts/strict-grep/orch-0957-no-metered-place-photo-reads.mjs`. Two emergency kill-switches preserved: `USE_PLACE_PHOTO_THUMBS=false` (top-level legacy revert) + the pre-existing ORCH-0737 v6 `DISABLE_PHOTO_URL_TRANSFORM=true` (panic-button override). One additional env `THUMB_404_FALLBACK_TO_TRANSFORM=true` (default) protects backfill window. **ID-collision resolution sub-decision:** ORCH-0957 was double-booked at INTAKE — the same ID had been REGISTERED 2026-05-24 for an unrelated bug (`spawn.sh` migration-timestamp collision detection). Operator chose 2026-05-25 to claim ORCH-0957 for this storage close (already shipped + branched + tested) and renumber the spawn.sh entry to ORCH-0960 (REGISTERED-only, no code, no worktree). COMMS-0004 written to warn the collision pattern for future INTAKEs; the orchestrator's intake rule should check WORLD_MAP for registered-but-not-yet-spawned IDs before claiming a "next free" number. Operator sign-off: "Tier b" (fix-tier choice) + "Claim 0957 for storage; renumber spawn.sh to ORCH-0960 (Recommended)" (ID-collision resolution).

> **2026-05-17 — DEC-160 + DEC-161 logged — ORCH-0855 [Tr1 Trip Planner Brand Onboarding] CLOSED PASS Grade A.** **DEC-160: PersonaPickerCards interface locked at 3 ids (`'place' | 'event' | 'trip'`).** The new `PersonaPickerCards` component in `mingla-business/src/components/brand/PersonaPickerCards.tsx` exports a `PersonaDef` interface whose `id` field is a literal union locked to exactly three values. Widening the union requires a new ORCH + SPEC + invariant amendment. Rationale: Tr1 (Track 1, Trip Planners) and Ve1 (Track 2, Physical Venues — different developer, parallel session) both build a persona fork in `BrandSwitcherSheet`. Without an explicit locked interface, Ve1 could fork the persona contract — drift, duplicated rendering, broken Ve1↔Tr1 compatibility. Codified as I-PROPOSED-TR1-PERSONA-INTERFACE (flipped DRAFT → ACTIVE at this close). Enforcement: tester adversarial check A-07 at `mingla-business/scripts/ci/orch-0855-adversarial-check.mjs` performs a banned-id widening attack (probes for `"venue"`, `"experience"`, `"creator"`, `"host"`, `"planner"` in the union); fails-on-revert verified at HEAD `7750f7d6` by temporarily widening to `"venue"`. EXIT condition: permanent until/unless a future ORCH explicitly adds a 4th persona kind. **DEC-161: `brands.kind` immutable post-create for `kind='trip_planner'`.** `BrandEditView.tsx` MUST NOT render the BRAND KIND editor for trip-planner brands; the legacy physical↔popup toggle for popup/physical brands MUST NOT include `'trip_planner'` as an option. Rationale: switching kind post-create breaks Stripe-status gating semantics, downstream analytics funnel assumptions, and trip-planner-specific UX prompts. Physical↔popup switching pre-dates this DEC and is grandfathered (low-risk because neither has Stripe-required-at-onboarding semantics). Codified as I-PROPOSED-TR1-KIND-IMMUTABLE (flipped DRAFT → ACTIVE at this close). Enforcement: tester adversarial A-13 verifies both `draft.kind !== "trip_planner"` guard wrapper AND no `setDraft({...kind: "trip_planner"...})` setter call. Trade-off accepted: a trip-planner host who later wants to demote cannot do so via the kind editor — they must create a fresh popup brand and migrate. Acceptable because trip-planner-down-to-popup is a rare path with downstream Stripe + analytics consequences; re-creating is reliable and audit-clean. EXIT condition: permanent. Both decisions are part of the Mingla Business 1.2 Track 1 milestone Tr1 — first trip-planner brand onboarding surface; downstream Tr2 [Minimum Viable Trip] depends on `kind='trip_planner'` rows existing in `brands` per DEC-160's locked persona id. Cross-references: SPEC `Mingla_Artifacts/specs/SPEC_ORCH-0855_TR1_TRIP_PLANNER_ONBOARDING.md` §8 + §14; implementation `reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0855_TR1_TRIP_PLANNER_ONBOARDING.md` §3.6 + §3.10; QA `reports/QA_ORCH-0855_TR1_TRIP_PLANNER_ONBOARDING_REPORT.md` §5 (A-07 + A-13); investigation `reports/INVESTIGATION_ORCH-0855_TR1_TRIP_PLANNER_ONBOARDING.md`; predecessor DECs: DEC-152 (ORCH-0826 [Hub Foundation + universal-plus creator] M0 TopSheet primitive that Tr1's BrandSwitcherSheet extension uses). Operator sign-off: "lets proceed with tr1" (INTAKE) + "I HAVE RUN SUPABASE DB PUSH" (Step 1 migration gate) + "yes fold it" then "no revert" (mid-session amendment proposed + reverted) + "my bad it actually works. Lets proceed" (live-fire confirmation).

> **2026-05-15 - DEC-158 logged - ORCH-0849 [Stripe payment-method parity across consumer + mingla-business] IMPLEMENTATION COMPLETE — bundled re-pivot per operator directive "we do them together to ensure parity" 2026-05-15.** Decision: (1) **Consumer (app-mobile) PaymentIntent payment_method_types expanded from card-only to a curated Phase 1 allowlist.** New shared module `supabase/functions/_shared/stripePaymentMethods.ts` exports `MINGLA_PM_ALLOWLIST = ["card","link","apple_pay","google_pay"] as const` + `getPaymentMethodTypes()` helper; edge function `ticket-checkout-create/index.ts:481` now sets `payment_method_types: [...getPaymentMethodTypes()]` via the spread call instead of the hardcoded `["card"]` literal. ORCH-0837 card-only constraint replaced; ORCH-0844's three load-bearing fixes (initStripe per-PI with stripeAccountId, Customer + ephemeralKey, withTimeout removal) make Apple Pay + Google Pay + Link safe to enable. Phase 2 methods (Cash App Pay, Klarna, Afterpay, ACH/SEPA, regional redirects) explicitly deferred to separate follow-up ORCHs that must prove redirect-flow / delayed-method plumbing per-method. (2) **mingla-business retires ORCH-0839-B Hosted Checkout pivot; adopts native PaymentSheet pattern verbatim from consumer.** `@stripe/stripe-react-native@^0.65.1` added to `mingla-business/package.json` (same version as `app-mobile`); Stripe plugin entry added to `mingla-business/app.json` with `merchantIdentifier: "merchant.com.mingla.business.v2"` + `enableGooglePay: true`; `<StripeNativeProvider merchantIdentifier urlScheme>` mounted at `mingla-business/app/_layout.tsx` root above the navigation tree; new `mingla-business/src/payments/nativeCheckoutFlow.ts` mirrors `app-mobile/src/payments/nativeCheckoutFlow.ts` verbatim (only differences: business supabase-client path, business merchantIdentifier/urlScheme values, inline edge-function-error extractor because mingla-business lacks the shared util); `mingla-business/app/checkout/[eventId]/payment.tsx` swapped from `expo-web-browser.openAuthSessionAsync` hosted-checkout flow to `useNativeCheckoutFlow` on native paths (iOS + Android); web path (Platform.OS === "web") retained on Hosted Checkout via `window.location.assign` because PaymentSheet doesn't render in browsers. (3) **PaymentElementStub.tsx deleted.** Dead `[TRANSITIONAL]` placeholder at `mingla-business/src/components/checkout/PaymentElementStub.tsx` removed; zero importers confirmed before deletion. (4) **CI gate work.** RETIRED `.github/scripts/strict-grep/orch-0839-b-mingla-business-no-native-stripe.mjs` (file deleted, workflow job + registry comment line replaced with retirement notice). AMENDED `app-mobile/scripts/ci/orch-0837-regression-check.mjs` T-C0 — was asserting hardcoded `["card"]` literal, now asserts `[...getPaymentMethodTypes()]` spread call + the helper import. T-C1 preserved verbatim. NEW `.github/scripts/strict-grep/i-stripe-pm-method-allowlist.mjs` — 6 rules covering allowlist export, edge fn import, spread call presence, hardcoded-literal absence, automatic_payment_methods absence, and exact Phase 1 method enumeration with Phase 2 method denylist. NEW `.github/scripts/strict-grep/i-stripe-paymentsheet-parity.mjs` — 8 rules covering provider mount + initStripe + customer/ephemeralKey on BOTH apps. Both new gates registered in `.github/workflows/strict-grep-mingla-business.yml` (jobs total: 75). (5) **Invariant changes.** AMENDED `I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-PI-EXPLICIT-METHOD-TYPES` (ORCH-0837) — was card-only, now "sourced from MINGLA_PM_ALLOWLIST allowlist constant". RETIRED `I-PROPOSED-MINGLA-BUSINESS-HOSTED-CHECKOUT-ONLY` (ORCH-0839-B per DEC-155 — superseded by parity invariant). NEW `I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-PAYMENTSHEET-PARITY` (top-level INVARIANT_REGISTRY entry) + NEW `I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-PM-METHOD-ALLOWLIST` (top-level entry), both DRAFT pending ORCH-0849 CLOSE. PRESERVED verbatim: all ORCH-0843 charge-shape invariants, all ORCH-0844 PaymentSheet stability invariants, ORCH-0837 I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-CALLBACK-WIRED. (6) **Regression tests.** Implementor happy-path at `supabase/functions/ticket-checkout-create/__tests__/payment_method_allowlist.test.ts` — 5 Deno tests, all PASS, fails-on-revert verified on TWO independent revert paths at HEAD commit `029e9cc552d9bdec59dd8b7965f2bdf5e4b12a6e` (path A: collapse allowlist to single-element constant; path B: replace edge-fn spread call with hardcoded card-only literal). Business parity test at `mingla-business/src/payments/__tests__/native_checkout_flow_parity.test.ts` — 8 Jest tests, all PASS, includes negative-control `expo-web-browser` import absence + `WebBrowser.openAuthSessionAsync` call absence. Tester-written adversarial regression test deferred to Claude `mingla-tester` TARGETED per SPEC §3.6.2. (7) **Operator-side ops prerequisites (assumptions A-1..A-4 from SPEC §2.3) are operator-verified separately from implementor work.** A-1: register `merchant.com.mingla.business.v2` in Stripe Dashboard with cert verification — REQUIRED before EAS rebuild + first Apple Pay live-fire on business; otherwise the Apple Pay row will be silently absent (NOT a crash post-ORCH-0844 — just absent). > **2026-05-15 - DEC-157 logged - ORCH-0844 [Explorer PaymentSheet — Connect account ID per-PI + 60s timeout removal] CLOSE — fixes iOS 26 "tried to resolve a promise more than once" double-resolve in the consumer (app-mobile) Stripe PaymentSheet flow. Two root causes resolved via small config deltas; Stripe Payment Sheet preserved as recommended pattern (operator pushback against pivot was correct).** Decision: (1) **R-1 missing `stripeAccountId` on mobile `initStripe`** — when ORCH-0843 flipped the charge architecture to direct charges (PaymentIntent now lives on the connected account via `Stripe-Account` header at edge function), the mobile SDK was never told which connected account to scope to. PaymentSheet's confirm step looked up the PI under the platform key → 404-style miss → SDK error path → double-resolve. Fix: extend `ticket-checkout-create` `requires_payment` response with `stripeAccountId`; call `initStripe({ publishableKey, stripeAccountId, merchantIdentifier, urlScheme })` per-PI in `nativeCheckoutFlow.ts` BEFORE `initPaymentSheet`. (2) **R-2 our own 60s `withTimeout` JS wrapper** in `useStripePaymentSheet.ts` was racing the native bridge promise. The synthetic timeout settled the JS Promise; iOS continued and emitted its real `resolve()` later → "tried to resolve a promise more than once". The original ORCH-0829-B hang the timeout was added for was already resolved by ORCH-0837's card-only PI restriction. Fix: remove the `withTimeout` wrapper for BOTH `initPaymentSheet` AND `presentPaymentSheet`; preserve the `inFlightInitRef` + `inFlightPresentRef` once-only guards (those handle JS-side double-invocation). (3) **A-3 saved payment methods via Customer + ephemeralKey** — Stripe's canonical PaymentSheet pattern uses CustomerSessions or ephemeralKey for saved-PM support. Edge function now creates a Connect-scoped idempotent Stripe Customer by buyer email (`stripe.customers.search` lookup, fallback to `stripe.customers.create` with idempotency key), generates an ephemeralKey via `stripe.ephemeralKeys.create({ customer, apiVersion: STRIPE_API_VERSION })`, returns both on `requires_payment`. Mobile passes `customer` + `customerEphemeralKeySecret` to `initPaymentSheet`. NON-FATAL guest-mode fallback if any of these Stripe API calls fail — purchase still works for one-off payments without saved PMs. (4) **A-4 drop `allowsDelayedPaymentMethods: false`** — redundant since PI is already `payment_method_types: ['card']` per ORCH-0837 (cards have no delayed variant). (5) **CI gate** — NEW `.github/scripts/strict-grep/orch-0844-stripe-connect-account-id-per-pi.mjs` with 4 sub-checks T-G1..T-G4 enforcing: T-G1 nativeCheckoutFlow.ts imports initStripe; T-G2 nativeCheckoutFlow.ts calls initStripe with stripeAccountId; T-G3 edge fn requires_payment response includes stripeAccountId; T-G4 edge fn response includes paired customerId + customerEphemeralKeySecret. Wired into `.github/workflows/strict-grep-mingla-business.yml` registry. (6) **Flipped gate** — `app-mobile/scripts/ci/orch-0829b-d1-regression-check.mjs` T-A6..T-A9 inverted to assert timeout-race ABSENCE (was asserting presence). (7) **Invariant changes** — NEW `I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-CONNECT-ACCOUNT-ID-PER-PI`; AMEND `I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-PROVIDER-FULL-CONFIG` (now also requires per-PI initStripe); RETIRE `I-PROPOSED-PAYMENT-SHEET-TIMEOUT-RACE`. PRESERVE `I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-PRESENT-ONCE-ONLY` (once-only guard stays), all ORCH-0837 invariants (card-only PI, handleURLCallback wired — H-1 hypothesis that handleURLCallback was the bug source was DISPROVEN in investigation), all ORCH-0843 invariants. (8) **Operator architectural pushback acknowledged** — earlier orchestrator session recommended pivoting explorer to Stripe Hosted Checkout (mirror of ORCH-0839-B). Operator correctly pushed back citing Stripe's official Payment Sheet documentation as the recommended pattern. The orchestrator's earlier reasoning was rushed and didn't exhaust Stripe's canonical config options (CustomerSessions, ephemeralKey, paymentMethodOrder, billingDetails). The pivot was based on incomplete investigation. This CLOSE preserves PaymentSheet and fixes via small config deltas — the correct outcome. Verdict CONDITIONAL PASS; operator chose to CLOSE + OTA + live-fire on real device. Cross-references: investigation `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/INVESTIGATION_ORCH-0844_EXPLORER_PAYMENTSHEET_DOUBLE_RESOLVE.md`; SPEC `Mingla_Artifacts/specs/SPEC_ORCH-0844_EXPLORER_PAYMENTSHEET_CONNECT_ACCOUNT_ID_AND_TIMEOUT_REMOVAL.md`; implementation `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0844_EXPLORER_PAYMENTSHEET_CONNECT_ACCOUNT_ID.md`; QA `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/QA_ORCH-0844_EXPLORER_PAYMENTSHEET_CONNECT_ACCOUNT_ID_REPORT.md`; Stripe RN GitHub issue #2464 cited in investigation as upstream context (config-dependent iOS 26 regression). > **2026-05-15 - DEC-156 logged - ORCH-0843 [Charge-Shape Reconciliation] CLOSE (REWORK PASS) — Path B shipped; DEC-154 (5) AMENDED to platform-managed risk; live ticket sales unblocked.** Amendment to DEC-154: ORCH-0843 flips Mingla's charge architecture from `transfer_data.destination` (destination charges, platform-liable but Stripe-rejected under Stripe-managed risk) to direct charges on the connected account with `Stripe-Account` request-option header + `application_fee_amount` (1.5% flat, hardcoded `Math.round(totalCents * 0.015)` in `ticket-checkout-create/index.ts` per operator G-2 decision) + `statement_descriptor_suffix: "MINGLA"` on the Checkout Session path (Stripe rejects `_prefix` at the per-PI level; a true `MINGLA*` prefix is a one-time account-level Dashboard config on Mingla's main platform account — queued as operator-side follow-up). This was caught by tester FAIL on first run (`surface: "web"` and `surface: "mobile-web"` returned HTTP 400) and resolved in a 5-edit REWORK before CLOSE. Risk/loss liability per DEC-154 (5) is now **platform-managed** (Mingla absorbs negative-balance losses including fraud-driven losses, chargebacks on the 120-day post-event tail, and event-cancellation-driven mass-refund balance gaps). REVERSES DEC-154 (5)'s original "Stripe-managed risk" stance. Re-onboarding test stubs to claim Stripe-managed-risk shield was busy work that delayed the live-sales unblocker. Operator accepts platform-liable chargeback risk as the conscious tradeoff to ship faster. Locks Mingla into platform-liable for the existing 9 accounts AND for all new accounts provisioned through the unchanged `stripeBlueprintClient.ts` until a future ORCH re-evaluates (sticky-controller-properties per DEC-154 (6) — re-evaluation requires detach + re-onboard of all platform-liable accounts). DEC-154 (2) (embedded onboarding) and DEC-154 (3) (embedded account management) become OPTIONAL — queued as deferred-priority future ORCHs. DEC-154 (7) hard-stop on live ticket sales IS LIFTED by this CLOSE; live sales open immediately on charges-enabled test brands subject to operator-side post-deploy monitoring (Phase 10.4 of SPEC). DEC-154 (9) security baselines (a-d) remain enforced: webhook signature verification VERIFIED (`stripe-webhook/index.ts:56` uses `verifyStripeWebhookSignature` on every event); event subscription set verified BUT `charge.dispute.created` is MISSING from `STRIPE_ROUTED_EVENT_TYPES` — under Path B platform-liable this is a P1 chargeback-blind-spot, filed as follow-up sub-ORCH (TBD ORCH-0843-FOLLOWUP-DISPUTES). Restricted API keys (`rk_live_*` per edge function) also queued as separate sub-ORCH. Four new invariants ACTIVE: I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-CHARGE-SHAPE-IS-DIRECT, I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-APPLICATION-FEE-PRESENT, I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-ACCOUNT-HEADER-ON-CONNECTED-CALLS, I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-STATEMENT-DESCRIPTOR-SUFFIX-MINGLA. New strict-grep CI gate `.github/scripts/strict-grep/orch-0843-stripe-direct-charges-only.mjs` with 6 sub-checks T-G1..T-G6 (T-G6 is the REWORK-added regression-prevention for `automatic_tax.liability.type: "account"` reintroduction). One existing gate relaxed: `orch-0804-stripe-tax-enabled-on-checkout.mjs` no longer requires `liability.account` (direct-charge contract). Verification: forensics TEST mode FAIL on first run (HTTP 400 on web + mobile-web surfaces from `automatic_tax.liability` Stripe rejection); REWORK applied 5 mechanical edits (drop `liability` block, flip test assertion, relax ORCH-0804 gate, add T-G6 regression-prevention, update probe); forensics RETEST mode CONDITIONAL PASS with FAIL→PASS transitions explicit on T-01 (web) + T-02 (mobile-web) + T-03 native no-regression + DB verification (75¢ fee correctly persisted) + adversarial T-G6 trip confirmed + 0 constitutional violations. Edge function deploys: `ticket-checkout-create v47` + `refund-order v26` (verified live via `mcp__supabase__list_edge_functions`). Transitional probe edge function `orch-0843-stripe-direct-charge-probe` deployed twice, deleted at CLOSE (deployed + local). Cross-references: SPEC `Mingla_Artifacts/specs/SPEC_ORCH-0843_CHARGE_SHAPE_RECONCILIATION.md` (note §3.1.3 SUPERSEDED by REWORK — Tax for Platforms direct-charge contract drops `liability` block); investigation `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/INVESTIGATION_ORCH-0843_CHARGE_SHAPE_RECONCILIATION.md`; implementation `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0843_CHARGE_SHAPE_RECONCILIATION.md` + REWORK `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0843_CHARGE_SHAPE_RECONCILIATION_REWORK.md`; QA `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/QA_ORCH-0843_CHARGE_SHAPE_RECONCILIATION_REPORT.md` (FAIL) + RETEST `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/QA_ORCH-0843_CHARGE_SHAPE_RECONCILIATION_REPORT_RETEST.md` (CONDITIONAL PASS). > **2026-05-15 - DEC-155 logged - ORCH-0839-B [Stripe Hosted Checkout pivot for mingla-business mobile] CLOSE — mingla-business mobile abandons native Stripe PaymentSheet for Stripe-hosted Checkout via `expo-web-browser.openAuthSessionAsync`; native Stripe SDK fully removed from mingla-business; web buyer flow unchanged; explorer (app-mobile) unchanged.** Decision: (1) **Native Stripe PaymentSheet is decommissioned in mingla-business.** `@stripe/stripe-react-native` is removed from `mingla-business/package.json`; the `@mingla/payments-native` Metro alias + tsconfig path are removed from mingla-business (Discovery 1 — the package was alias-resolved, not npm-installed); the Stripe plugin entry is removed from `mingla-business/app.config.ts`; the entire `mingla-business/src/payments/` directory is deleted (7 files: `StripeNativeProvider.{native,web,}.tsx`, `normalizePaymentSheetResult.ts`, `stripePaymentSheet.{native,web,}.ts`). `StripeNativeProvider` wrapping in `_layout.tsx` is collapsed to a no-op pass-through. The native Stripe SDK is therefore no longer in the mingla-business binary, eliminating the iOS 26 + newArch + bridgeless TurboModule fragility documented in `INVESTIGATION_ORCH-0833-0834-RESCOPED`. The shared `packages/payments-native/` source is untouched — `app-mobile` (explorer) continues to consume it for its `surface: "native"` PaymentSheet path. (2) **Mobile checkout uses `expo-web-browser.openAuthSessionAsync` to open Stripe-hosted Checkout in-app.** The buyer taps Pay → `WebBrowser.openAuthSessionAsync(stripeHostedUrl, returnUrl)` opens an in-app ASWebAuthenticationSession (iOS) / Custom Tabs (Android) → user pays on Stripe's hosted page (with Apple Pay / Google Pay / Card / Klarna / Affirm rendered by Stripe) → browser dismisses on success or cancel → app reads the return URL → routes to confirmation or back to /payment. The mobile flow mirrors the existing web buyer flow byte-identically; the only platform-specific difference is the browser primitive. The pattern is proven elsewhere in the same app (`BrandOnboardView.tsx:362` for Stripe Connect onboarding). (3) **Edge function `ticket-checkout-create` gains a third surface discriminator `"mobile-web"`** (additive, backward-compatible) that emits `success_url` + `cancel_url` with the `mingla-business://checkout/return` custom URI scheme. The pre-existing `"web"` surface (emits `https://...` URLs for web buyers) and `"native"` surface (emits PaymentIntents for the explorer app's PaymentSheet path) are byte-unchanged. Backward-compat is mandatory and was verified live: edge function v43 deployed; `"web"` + `"native"` + `"mobile-web"` all proven against a real Stripe-Connect-enabled test event. (4) **Stripe accepts custom-scheme `success_url`/`cancel_url`.** A one-shot probe edge function `orch-0839-b-stripe-probe` was deployed to `https://gqnoajqerqhnvulmnyvv.supabase.co/functions/v1/orch-0839-b-stripe-probe`, invoked `https://api.stripe.com/v1/checkout/sessions` with `success_url=mingla-business://checkout/return?cs={CHECKOUT_SESSION_ID}` + `cancel_url=mingla-business://checkout/return?status=cancel`, received HTTP 200 with a valid `cs_test_*` ID, and confirmed Stripe persists `cancel_url: "mingla-business://checkout/return?status=cancel"` verbatim. Decision gate satisfied. SPEC §11 https-bridge fallback (a public 302-redirector at `https://business.usemingla.com/checkout/return`) is therefore NOT exercised. Probe function deleted at CLOSE (deployed + local). (5) **Four mingla-business-side invariants RETIRE; they remain ACTIVE for app-mobile.** I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-ERROR-CODE-DISCRIMINATED (no Stripe error codes to discriminate when there's no Stripe SDK), I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-PROVIDER-FULL-CONFIG (no `<StripeProvider>` to configure), I-PROPOSED-PAYMENT-SHEET-TIMEOUT-RACE (no PaymentSheet to race), I-PROPOSED-STRIPE-PRESENT-ONCE-ONLY (no native sheet presentation to dedupe). These are decommissioned-in-context, NOT globally dropped — `app-mobile/src/payments/nativeCheckoutFlow.ts` still uses the native PaymentSheet via `@mingla/payments-native` and is governed by all four invariants unchanged. (6) **Two new invariants are ACTIVE.** I-PROPOSED-MOBILE-WEB-SURFACE-RETURNS-CUSTOM-SCHEME (`ticket-checkout-create` MUST emit `mingla-business://checkout/return` for `surface="mobile-web"` and `https://...` for `surface="web"`; codified in the new gate T-G8). I-PROPOSED-MINGLA-BUSINESS-NO-NATIVE-STRIPE (mingla-business code MUST NOT import from `@stripe/stripe-react-native` or `@mingla/payments-native` — 8 contracts T-G1..T-G8 enforced by `.github/scripts/strict-grep/orch-0839-b-mingla-business-no-native-stripe.mjs` wired into `.github/workflows/strict-grep-mingla-business.yml`). (7) **Three pre-existing strict-grep gates are retired in mingla-business while staying ACTIVE elsewhere.** ORCH-0789 §4 + §5 (Stripe error-toast-dismissible) retired in `orch-0789-error-toast-dismissible.mjs`; ORCH-0778 mingla-business `allowedNativeImportFiles` Set shrunk to empty; ORCH-0777 `presentPaymentSheet` assertion (structurally invalidated by the pivot) retired in `orch-0777-ticket-checkout-production.mjs`. The invariants underpinning each (web-export gating, ticket-checkout integration) remain enforced for the surfaces that still consume native Stripe (app-mobile, edge function). (8) **expo-doctor hygiene shipped opportunistically as part of this CLOSE.** 4 pre-existing issues resolved: renamed `tsc` package.json script to `typecheck` (conflicted with `node_modules/.bin/tsc`); patch-version bumps for `expo` (54.0.33 → 54.0.34), `expo-dev-client` (6.0.20 → 6.0.21), `expo-file-system` (19.0.21 → 19.0.22), `expo-linking` (8.0.11 → 8.0.12), `expo-web-browser` (15.0.10 → 15.0.11); `react-native-nfc-manager` added to `expo.doctor.reactNativeDirectoryCheck.exclude` (new-arch untested warning silenced); `channel: "production"` added to all `eas.json` build profiles (EAS Update channel warning resolved). Two new EAS profiles added: `development-sim` + `preview-sim` (iOS sim-targeted variants of dev/preview, both with `ios.simulator: true`). 17/17 expo-doctor checks now pass. (9) **THIS CLOSE DOES NOT UNBLOCK LIVE SALES.** The DEC-154 / ORCH-0841 hard stop on live ticket sales remains in force unchanged: `ticket-checkout-create` still uses `transfer_data: { destination: stripeAccountId }` (destination charges, platform-liable) which is incompatible with the locked Stripe-managed-risk model. ORCH-0839-B is purely the mobile UX pivot and does not address the charge architecture. Sub-ORCH (a) per DEC-154 (charge-shape reconciliation: flip from `transfer_data.destination` to direct charges with `application_fee_amount`) is mandatory and is dispatched as the immediate next ORCH after this CLOSE. (10) **EAS OTA is NOT APPLICABLE for this work.** Removing a native module + Expo plugin entry requires a fresh native build — not OTA-able. EAS preview builds (sim-targeted, both platforms) were used for QA on `Build.app` (iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.4) and `mingla-business-preview.apk`; iOS live-fire fully proven; Android live-fire blocked environmentally (Pixel_8_Pro AVD on API 37 vs APK targetSdk 36 manifest mismatch — NOT a code defect; physical-device Android QA queued for the production rebuild pre-store submission per standard release process). Production EAS rebuild + App Store / Play Store submission for mingla-business are also queued post-merge. Rationale: implementation passed via Claude `mingla-forensics` TEST mode (TARGETED sub-mode) with verdict CONDITIONAL PASS (P0:0 P1:0 P2:0 P3:1 P4:2) on 2026-05-15; iOS live-fire proven end-to-end including Pay → SFAuthenticationSession consent → Stripe-hosted page rendered with correct merchant (MINGLA LLC [Sandbox]) + amount ($50.00 USD) + email + methods (Apple Pay + Card + Klarna + Affirm); cancel path verified (browser dismiss → app returns to /payment → no order created → DB session stays at `awaiting_web_redirect` 15-min expiry); explorer backward-compat verified (`surface: "native"` against deployed v43 returns PaymentIntent identical to v42); web parity preserved; constitutional 14-rule audit zero violations; new gate `orch-0839-b-mingla-business-no-native-stripe.mjs` negative-probed (correctly trips on a one-line StripeProvider reintroduction). Cross-references: SPEC `Mingla_Artifacts/specs/SPEC_ORCH-0839-B_STRIPE_HOSTED_CHECKOUT_PIVOT.md` (764 lines, 12 SC, 13 test cases); implementation `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0839-B_STRIPE_HOSTED_CHECKOUT_PIVOT.md`; QA `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/QA_ORCH-0839-B_STRIPE_HOSTED_CHECKOUT_PIVOT_REPORT.md`; predecessor investigation `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/INVESTIGATION_ORCH-0833-0834-RESCOPED_STRIPE_CONFIG_AND_ALL_FILTER_NO_TM.md`; the four retiring invariants stay ACTIVE in INVARIANT_REGISTRY scoped to app-mobile; DEC-154 / ORCH-0841 live-sales hard stop unchanged. Operator sign-off: chat instructions "TAKE OVER" + "i have merged lets proceed" + "lets proceed" + "Yes rebuild for both sims and development builds, android and ios" + "Accept CONDITIONAL → proceed to CLOSE" + "CLOSE ORCH-0839-B now → immediately dispatch the charge-shape ORCH next" 2026-05-14/15 (mingla-orchestrator skill session, Claude side).

> **2026-05-15 - DEC-154 logged - ORCH-0841 [Stripe live Connect activation — Accounts v2 controller-properties locked] REGISTER — Mingla's live Stripe Connect platform is activated with embedded onboarding + embedded account management + Stripe-managed loss liability + direct charges on connected accounts; Mingla owns `application_fee_amount` as platform revenue, Stripe owns MoR for buyer-creator funds + KYC + negative-balance losses.** Decision: (1) **Funds flow = direct charges on the connected account, with `application_fee_amount` for Mingla's platform cut.** Buyer's card statement descriptor will show the *creator's* business name (with optional `statement_descriptor_prefix` for `MINGLA*` prefix). NOT destination charges via `transfer_data.destination` (incompatible with Stripe-managed risk + locks Mingla out of the Stripe-liable model). (2) **Account creation = embedded onboarding components (`@stripe/connect-js` or React Native equivalent), NOT Stripe-hosted redirect.** Creator never leaves the Mingla brand during KYC. Stripe still renders the forms (KYC, IDV, bank-account linkage, business-info collection) and Stripe still owns local KYC compliance + country/language coverage — Mingla just hosts the UI shell. Tradeoff accepted: implementor work to wire embedded components into mingla-business + lifecycle-handle embedded session tokens. (3) **Account management = embedded account-management components, NOT the standalone Express Dashboard redirect.** Same brand-continuity reasoning: creator manages payouts, taxes, account details inside mingla-business via embedded surfaces. `controller.stripe_dashboard.type = none` with embedded surfaces in lieu. (4) **Pricing owner = Stripe.** Stripe charges the connected account for processing fees; Mingla's platform revenue rides on top via `application_fee_amount` and is reconciled separately. Creator sees Stripe processing fees as their own cost; Mingla's take is itemized as platform fee. (5) **Risk and loss liability = Stripe-managed.** Stripe absorbs negative-balance losses on connected accounts, including fraud-driven losses. Mingla is NOT liable as backstop for creator chargebacks, event cancellations, or fraud. This is the lowest-liability shape Stripe offers for a marketplace and is the right call for Mingla's stage (early, US-only, anonymous-buyer ticketing with 120-day post-event chargeback tail in a "high-risk vertical"). (6) **Controller properties are sticky after first connected-account creation.** Once a brand onboards under this controller shape, flipping to platform-liable losses or destination charges requires creating new connected accounts and re-onboarding. Pick-once decision; this DEC locks it in. (7) **Hard stop on live sales through ORCH-0839-B [Stripe hosted Checkout pivot] code.** The existing `supabase/functions/ticket-checkout-create/index.ts` + `mingla-business/src/services/ticketCheckoutService.ts` must be forensics-investigated to verify the Checkout Session uses direct charges with `application_fee_amount` (Stripe-managed-risk-compatible) and NOT `transfer_data.destination`. If the code is wrong, SPEC the flip BEFORE any live ticket sale. Tracked as a sub-ORCH queued under ORCH-0841 in the World Map banner. (8) **Embedded components is a deviation from the prior orchestrator recommendation (which was Stripe-hosted redirect for minimum coding effort).** Operator chose embedded for brand continuity. Recorded honestly: this is a more expensive integration than the originally-recommended shape, but loss-liability and KYC-compliance burden are identical to the hosted option. (9) **Security baselines that ride on top of this DEC** — must be addressed in subsequent ORCHs before the first live sale: (a) use restricted API keys (`rk_live_*`) per edge function, never the secret key; (b) verify webhook signatures on every Stripe event handler; (c) configure webhook endpoint for at minimum `account.updated`, `account.application.authorized`, `checkout.session.completed`, `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.created`, `payout.failed`; (d) creator-facing copy must disclose three Stripe-managed-risk realities upfront — statement descriptor shows creator's business name, payouts may be delayed for future-dated events per ticketing risk band, refunds debit creator balance first with Stripe absorbing any resulting negative balance. Rationale: operator-direct decision in mingla-orchestrator + stripe-best-practices skill consult on 2026-05-15; Mingla Business strategy in memory (Cycle B2 Stripe Connect → B3 Checkout → B4 Scanner) ratifies marketplace shape; Stripe `references/connect.md` codifies Accounts v2 controller-properties as the modern path; Stripe `references/security.md` mandates Stripe-managed risk as the default for marketplaces unless a specific platform-managed-risk justification exists (Mingla has none). Cross-references: `Mingla_Artifacts/WORLD_MAP.md` ORCH-0841 registration banner 2026-05-15; `Mingla_Artifacts/specs/SPEC_ORCH-0839-B_STRIPE_HOSTED_CHECKOUT_PIVOT.md`; Stripe Connect Platform Agreement accepted at activation 2026-05-15. Operator sign-off: chat instruction "i chose this register it" 2026-05-15 with the five-item activation summary verbatim.

> **2026-05-14 - DEC-153 logged - ORCH-0840 [Regression-test enforcement + append-only CI] CLOSE — every code-touching ORCH now ships two regression tests (implementor happy-path + tester adversarial), tests are append-only at the CI layer, and CODEOWNERS auto-requests operator review on every test-file change.** Decision: (1) **Regression tests are MANDATORY on every code-touching CLOSE.** The orchestrator's CLOSE protocol gains a new Step 0.5 (gates BEFORE Step 1 artifact updates) that REJECTS CLOSE unless the tester report cites both an implementor-written happy-path regression test (with `fails-on-revert verified at <commit hash>` line) AND a tester-written adversarial regression test attacking a different angle (edge case / boundary / error path / malformed input / race condition / invariant violation). A copy of the implementor's test with a renamed `it()` block triggers a P1 finding — NOT acceptable as the adversarial test. Codified as new invariant **I-REGRESSION-TEST-MANDATORY** in the registry. (2) **Pragmatic Append-Only on test files at the CI layer.** New CI gate `.github/workflows/tests-append-only.yml` (required check on PR to `main` + push to `Seth`) runs `.github/scripts/test-append-only-check.js`. Rules: added test files → ALLOWED; deleted test files → FAIL (no override possible); renamed test files → FAIL unless commit body cites `[TEST-RENAME-APPROVED ORCH-NNNN]`; modified test files with deleted lines → FAIL unless commit body cites `[TEST-MOD-APPROVED ORCH-NNNN]`; modified test files with additions only → ALLOWED. Override tokens must appear verbatim in the HEAD commit body (Adv 3 design: rebase-resistant; tokens in earlier commits do NOT count). Codified as new invariant **I-TESTS-APPEND-ONLY**. (3) **BACKFILL-EXEMPT escape valve.** Pure docs / artifact / orchestration / process closes with ZERO product-code touch (no diff in `app-mobile/src/`, `mingla-business/src/`, `mingla-admin/src/`, `supabase/functions/`, `packages/`, `.github/scripts/strict-grep/`) may state `BACKFILL-EXEMPT — reason: <one sentence>` in the CLOSE banner. ORCH-0840 itself qualifies for this exemption (pure CI + skill-file process change); this CLOSE is the gate's first real exercise of its own exemption clause and confirms the escape valve fires correctly. (4) **CODEOWNERS-based human-in-the-loop accountability.** New `.github/CODEOWNERS` auto-requests Seth's review on every PR touching `**/*.test.*`, `**/*.spec.*`, `**/__tests__/**`, AND on changes to the append-only gate infrastructure itself (`.github/scripts/test-append-only-check.js`, `.github/workflows/tests-append-only.yml`, `.github/CODEOWNERS`). CI enforces the mechanical rule; CODEOWNERS ensures every authorized modification is reviewed. (5) **Forward + opportunistic backfill rollout strategy.** Policy applies to ORCHs closed from 2026-05-14 onward; existing untested legacy code is grandfathered. New informational warning gate `.github/scripts/strict-grep/regression-test-backfill-warning.mjs` (registered as `I-REGRESSION-TEST-BACKFILL-WARN` in `strict-grep-mingla-business.yml`) lists modified-but-untested source files when a PR touches them; warning-only, never blocks. Drives incremental backfill rather than big-bang. (6) **Implementor's `fails-on-revert` step is non-negotiable.** Implementor writes the regression test, runs it green, then reverts the fix locally (`git stash`) and re-runs the test — it MUST FAIL. Then restore and confirm PASS. Cites path + commit-hash-before-fix in the implementation report. This eliminates the "test passes regardless of the fix" failure mode that plagued ORCH-0810/0812 style work. (7) **Tester writes a SECOND, adversarial regression test on top of the implementor's.** Not a copy — a genuinely different angle. Both must appear in `git diff origin/main...HEAD --name-only` of the closing PR (no merging tests in from a side branch). (8) **Skill-file mandates are mirrored into INVARIANT_REGISTRY.md for repo-side visibility.** The three skill files at `.claude/skills/{mingla-orchestrator,mingla-tester,mingla-implementor}/SKILL.md` are gitignored (`.gitignore` line 41: "AI tool configs (private)"), so the in-skill mandates govern only Seth's local Claude/Codex sessions. The new INVARIANT_REGISTRY entries `I-REGRESSION-TEST-MANDATORY` and `I-TESTS-APPEND-ONLY` are the public mirror that makes the rules auditable to anyone reading the repo. Rationale: operator directive 2026-05-14 ("every single closed code to have regressions that make it impossible to break when we move forward — the only way to change the test is to write a new test, and i want it enforced on the code base"); tester verified 11 adversarial scenarios PASS on the append-only script; 5-of-5 implementor smoke scenarios PASS; 4 existing strict-grep gates sampled clean against the new `paths:` filter extension. Verdict PASS via tester (0 P0, 0 P1, 1 P2, 1 P3, 2 P4). Six discoveries surfaced for follow-up: ORCH-0839-B uncommitted work intermixed (handled at this CLOSE by selectively staging ORCH-0840 hunks only), P2-001 skill-file privacy (addressed by this DEC entry + the registry mirror), P3-001 pre-existing `i-proposed-x-web-deprecation.mjs` silent-error (deferred to a separate follow-up ORCH), branch-protection update needed (Seth Case-B step), Codex `implementor-mingla` mirror update needed (deferred to a META-ORCH), Jest wiring missing in `app-mobile/` + `mingla-admin/` (deferred to a wiring ORCH before first non-exempt CLOSE in those domains). > **2026-05-14 - DEC-152 logged - ORCH-0826 M0 Hub Foundation CLOSE — Mingla Business 1.2 unified offering model is now schema-backed and the universal "+" creator pattern is live across four tab surfaces.** Decision: (1) **`public.events.event_type` is the unified-offering discriminator** (text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'event' CHECK IN ('event','experience','trip')) — every sellable thing in Mingla Business 1.2 lives as a row in `events` with `event_type` distinguishing today's ticketed events (popup organizers, default `'event'`), single-intent venue experiences (Ve5+, `'experience'`), and multi-day curated trip packages (Tr2+, `'trip'`). Establishes new invariant **I-1.2-UNIFIED-EVENT-TYPE** — no parallel offering tables. Migration `supabase/migrations/20260605000000_orch_0826_events_event_type_discriminator.sql` is monotonic past head `20260604000004`, defensively backfills via explicit UPDATE on top of `NOT NULL DEFAULT`, and runs a `DO $$ … RAISE NOTICE/EXCEPTION $$` self-verification block that fails the migration if any row ends up NULL or invalid post-backfill. Applied live on 2026-05-14 (operator ran `supabase db push --linked`; NOTICE confirmed). (2) **The bottom-nav second tab is renamed Events → Hub** and houses three sub-routes (Events / Experiences / Trips) via a sticky `HubSubNav` pill row. `app/(tabs)/hub/index.tsx` Redirects `/hub` → `/hub/events` so the Hub tab lands deterministically on Events. Hard rename: zero internal callers of `/(tabs)/events` remain; legacy route deleted (no stub redirect). (3) **The TopBar is owned by `hub/_layout.tsx`** above the HubSubNav pill row — every Hub sub-route is content-only, no nested TopBar. This M0-rework decision came after operator live-fire smoke flagged a duplicate "Events" header rendering below the sub-tab pills; the architecture now mirrors `marketing/_layout.tsx`. Brand switcher state (BrandSwitcherSheet + BrandDeleteSheet) and universal creator state both live in the Hub layout. (4) **TopSheet primitive usage is extended beyond BrandSwitcherSheet to UniversalCreatorSheet** (supersedes-extends DEC-080 carve-out). Two acceptable consumers now: BrandSwitcherSheet (legacy, `heightMode="fixed-70"` default) and UniversalCreatorSheet (M0, `heightMode="compact"`). Future additional TopSheet consumers still require orchestrator approval + a new DEC entry. (5) **TopSheet gains a new additive `heightMode: "fixed-70" | "compact"` prop.** `"fixed-70"` (default) preserves byte-equivalent BrandSwitcherSheet behavior at 70% of screen height. `"compact"` measures content via `onLayout` and sizes the panel to fit. Implementation detail codified at CLOSE after a P0 chicken-and-egg surfaced during live smoke: setting `height: 0` pre-measurement clipped children to 0, so `onLayout` never reported a non-zero height and the sheet stayed invisible (only the scrim rendered). Resolution: pre-measurement, the panel renders at NATURAL content height with `opacity: 0`; post-measurement, panel switches to the explicit measured height with `opacity: 1` and normal animation. (6) **The universal "+" creator is present on Home, Hub, Marketing, and Account; absent on Ari; auto-hidden on `/marketing/campaigns/compose`.** Rationale: the "+" must travel everywhere a brand operator could plausibly start a create flow, except inside Ari (the AI agent has its own create-via-chat affordance) and inside the marketing composer (focused-authoring surface — matches the existing BottomNav hide pattern). On Hub specifically, the "+" lives in the layout TopBar so it's visible across Events / Experiences / Trips sub-tabs uniformly. (7) **All `/(tabs)/events` route references hard-renamed to `/(tabs)/hub/events`** across 6 caller files (home.tsx, event/[id]/edit, event/[id]/index, event/[id]/preview, PublicEventPage, eventCoverMedia.test.ts) plus the moved file itself. `routes.ts` updated to expose `hub`, `hubEvents`, `hubExperiences`, `hubTrips`, `marketing`, `ari` keys; legacy `events` key removed. (8) **In the Hub > Events sub-route, the filter pills (All / Live / Upcoming / Drafts / Past) are siblings of the events ScrollView, not children** — they stay fixed at the top while the list scrolls beneath. Pre-M0, the pills were nested inside the same vertical ScrollView and scrolled away with the list; operator-flagged during smoke. Rationale: live-fire QA verdict CONDITIONAL PASS post-migration apply + iOS Simulator smoke pass (Hub lands on Events, single TopBar above sub-tab pills, "+" sheet renders, brand switcher unchanged, filter pills sticky). Three independent gates green: tsc clean on M0 files, jest no-new-regressions, strict-grep I-37/I-38/I-39 (196 .tsx files scanned, 0 M0 violations). Evidence chain: investigation `reports/INVESTIGATION_ORCH-0826_M0_HUB_FOUNDATION.md`, spec `specs/SPEC_ORCH-0826_M0_HUB_FOUNDATION.md`, implementation `reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0826_M0_HUB_FOUNDATION.md`, QA `reports/QA_ORCH-0826_M0_HUB_FOUNDATION.md`. Deploy notes: migration is LIVE on remote (operator ran `supabase db push --linked` 2026-05-14); no edge function changes; EAS OTA via `eas update --branch production --platform ios` then `--platform android` from `mingla-business/` ships the JS bundle. Operator + orchestrator CLOSE protocol 2026-05-14 (mingla-orchestrator skill session, Claude side; operator delegated "take over" three times).

> **2026-05-13 - DEC-151 logged - ORCH-0823 CLOSE — Event Wizard `Big␣⇪P` autocorrect-and-capslock bug fixed via centralised `VARIANT_BEHAVIOUR` rewrite; Path A discovered during TEST mode after Path B was eliminated; spec SC-9 superseded.** Decision: (1) **iOS UIKit autocorrect (`autoCorrect: true` default) is banned on every Mingla `Input` variant** — `autoCorrect=true` produces near-miss smart-replacement substitutions (proven: typed `Big P` on the broken `text` variant rendered as `BigP` with a `Bigot` suggestion bubble; iOS visually compresses the space). The centralised `VARIANT_BEHAVIOUR` table in `mingla-business/src/components/ui/Input.variants.ts` now sets `autoCorrect: false` on all six variants (text, email, phone, number, password, search). (2) **iOS UIKit `autoCapitalize: "sentences"` is banned on every Mingla `Input` variant + on the wizard's raw Description `<TextInput>`** — sentences-mode primes a pre-capitalize state machine that collides with hardware caps-lock keypresses: pressing caps lock while a pending trailing space exists in the buffer causes iOS to silently delete the trailing space before any subsequent character is processed. Proven by patched-build QA evidence `Mingla_Artifacts/evidence/ORCH-0823-test/T01-CLEAN-3.png` (field shows `Big` with cursor at "g" — no space gap — immediately after capslock keypress, before any letter). The v2 fix replaces `"sentences"` with `"none"` everywhere ORCH-0823 touches. (3) **The original investigation report wrongly ruled out Path A.** The no-capslock control test (r-6) produced the same visible `BigP`+`Bigot` output as the with-capslock case (r-4), and the investigator concluded autocorrect alone was sufficient and capslock was incidental. Path B's autocorrect smart-replacement was actually MASKING Path A's space erasure; eliminating Path B by setting `autoCorrect=false` unmasked Path A as a real, independent defect. The investigation report now carries an errata addendum at the bottom citing this DEC entry and the T01-CLEAN-3 evidence. (4) **Spec SC-9 ("first-letter auto-capitalize still works on Event name") is formally SUPERSEDED.** The original spec chose `autoCapitalize: "sentences"` to preserve first-letter auto-cap; Path A's discovery made `"sentences"` impossible. The replacement criterion: typed `slow burn vol. 4` MUST persist as `slow burn vol. 4` exactly (no auto-cap). Verified PASS via Maestro live-fire `T07-no-autocap.png`. Operator implicitly authorized the SC-9 drop via "take over" dispatch on the v2 rework after seeing the layman trade-off in chat. (5) **New invariant I-PROPOSED-BP INPUT-VARIANT-EXPLICIT-FLAGS** registered as ACTIVE — every Input variant must declare both `autoCorrect` and `autoCapitalize`; `autoCorrect` must be `false`; `autoCapitalize` must NOT be `"sentences"`. Enforced by Jest regression test `Input.variantBehaviour.test.tsx` (30 assertions, sanity-check verified). (6) **Test-tooling pivot codified mid-RETEST** — `osascript`-driven simulator tests steal macOS keyboard focus and are operator-hostile; Maestro (via `~/.maestro/bin/maestro` + brew openjdk@25) is the new default for sim-driven QA where keyboard-focus theft is a concern; fb-idb (broken on Python 3.14 in operator's env) reserved as the hardware-keyboard-event fallback. (7) **Process improvement:** future investigations of keypress-interaction bugs MUST run a "modifier-key isolation matrix" — explicitly test the bug WITH and WITHOUT each modifier (caps lock, shift, option) under each candidate fix combination. Trusting same-visible-output as "modifier ruled out" caused this ORCH to ship a v1 patch that only solved half the problem. Rationale: live-fire QA via Maestro on iPhone 17 Pro UDID `17091E60-C3B6-4167-980D-60C348E177F6`; PASS verdict P0:0 P1:0 P2:0 P3:0 P4:2; 30/30 regression tests; tsc clean; lint clean; 26 `<Input variant="text">` consumers across 11 files inherit centralised fix. Cross-references: investigation `reports/INVESTIGATION_ORCH-0823_EVENT_WIZARD_SPACE_CAPSLOCK_GLITCH.md` (with errata addendum); spec `specs/SPEC_ORCH-0823_EVENT_WIZARD_SPACE_CAPSLOCK_GLITCH.md` (SC-9 superseded); v1 implementation `reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0823_EVENT_WIZARD_SPACE_CAPSLOCK_GLITCH.md`; v1 QA FAIL `reports/QA_ORCH-0823_EVENT_WIZARD_SPACE_CAPSLOCK_GLITCH_REPORT.md`; v2 implementation `reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0823_EVENT_WIZARD_SPACE_CAPSLOCK_GLITCH_v2.md`; v2 RETEST PASS `reports/QA_ORCH-0823_EVENT_WIZARD_SPACE_CAPSLOCK_GLITCH_RETEST_REPORT.md`; close note `CLOSE_NOTE_ORCH-0823.md`. Operator + orchestrator CLOSE protocol 2026-05-13 (mingla-orchestrator skill session, Claude side, operator delegated "take over").

> **2026-05-12 - DEC-150 logged - ORCH-0809 + ORCH-0809-D + ORCH-0809-E CLOSE — Discover Ticketmaster filter expansion v1 shipped end-to-end with seven architectural rules codified.** Decision: (1) **Mingla's Discover screen owns the user's chosen city as a server-side preference, not as Zustand client state** — five new `discover_city_*` columns on `public.preferences` (name, state code, country code, lat, lng) persist the city across sign-out. GPS-derived city is the chip's default ONLY when `discover_city_name IS NULL`. Reference component: `app-mobile/src/components/discover/CityPickerSheet.tsx`. Codified as **I-PROPOSED-BL DISCOVER_CITY_PERSISTED**. (2) **Mingla's Discover screen uses 4 real Ticketmaster segments — not the original SPEC's 6-segment plan** — Music / Sports / Arts & Theatre / Film. Comedy and Family demoted to genre-level inside Arts & Theatre and Film respectively, because TM does not model them as top-level segments. The original SPEC §2 was wrong about TM's structure; this CLOSE corrects it. (3) **Server owns all Ticketmaster classification IDs (segment + genre + sub-genre); client ships only slugs.** Edge function rejects unknown `segmentSlug` with HTTP 400 + `supported` list rather than silently falling back to Music. Reference helper: `supabase/functions/_shared/ticketmasterClassifications.ts` `resolveTmClassification`. Codified as **I-PROPOSED-BM DISCOVER_TM_CLASSIFICATION_BY_ID**. (4) **Discover date chips compute in device-local time via TM's `localStartEndDateTime` parameter, not UTC `startDateTime`/`endDateTime`.** This fixes Tonight/Weekend/Next Week/This Month drifting around midnight in non-UTC timezones. Codified as **I-PROPOSED-BN DISCOVER_TM_LOCAL_TIME_WINDOWS**. (5) **The first reusable curated-union chip pattern is in place** — a single slug ("afro") can map to `{ genreId, subGenreIds[] }` so the edge function fans out to multiple TM sub-genres in a single request via `subGenreId=ID1,ID2,...`. The "Afro" chip unions 9 TM sub-genres under World music — live-fire against TM returned 17 real US events in a single response. Pattern extends to future Reggaeton / K-Pop / Dancehall / House / Techno / Bachata chips at ~5 LOC each. (6) **The price filter is permanently removed from Discover until Mingla Business native events with structured pricing are integrated.** Ticketmaster's Discovery API has no price query parameter; the pre-ORCH-0809 client-side post-filter silently hid events with no UX signal (Constitution #3 + #9 violation). Reintroduction is a separate future ORCH tied to Mingla Business event integration into Discover. (7) **Process invariant emerging from six iterations within ORCH-0809:** any user-selectable filter dimension MUST either be validated at the server boundary OR explicitly degrade with a user-visible signal — never silently fall through to a default — AND MUST appear in every cache key on every layer (AsyncStorage + React Query + server cache). Codified as DRAFT **I-PROPOSED-BO FILTER_DIMENSION_VALIDATED_OR_KEYED**; flips ACTIVE on the next SPEC that explicitly references it. **Invariant ID collision flagged during CLOSE:** ORCH-0815 SPEC reserved I-PROPOSED-BF through I-PROPOSED-BK for channel-extensibility invariants, but BF and BG were already in the live registry (Stripe Tax + Brand Avatar). Discover renumbered its planned BH/BI/BJ/BK to BL/BM/BN/BO to land cleanly; **ORCH-0815 SPEC must renumber its BF + BG before IMPLEMENT dispatch.** Rationale: operator-witnessed end-to-end across 4 live-test phases ("works perfect" / "all works"); 23/23 Deno tests + 10/10 mobile regression checks + 3 strict-grep CI gates with negative-control proofs (17 checks total) + tsc clean; edge function deployed at 88.58kB; 6 EAS OTAs across iOS + Android; TM API live-fire returned 17 real Afro events. Cross-references: SPEC `specs/SPEC_ORCH-0809_DISCOVER_TICKETMASTER_FILTER_EXPANSION_V1.md`, implementation reports `reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0809_*_M1/M2/M2_1/M3.md`, audit `reports/QA_ORCH-0809_PRE_M3_AUDIT_REPORT.md` §13. Operator + orchestrator CLOSE protocol 2026-05-12 (mingla-orchestrator skill session, Claude side).

> **2026-05-12 - DEC-149 logged - Marketing Hub is dual-surfaced (standalone "Marketing" tab + contextual entry points from event and brand); brand-level "Customers" tab is the canonical audience-source UI.** Decision: (1) The Cycle B5 Marketing Hub gets a top-level "Marketing" tab on the `mingla-business` bottom navigation, NOT only an embedded surface. The standalone tab is the full workspace (Overview, Audiences, Campaigns, Templates, Settings) and is the home for cross-brand rollups, suppression-list management, the template library, and analytics that span more than one event. (2) In addition to the standalone tab, the hub has two contextual entry points: a "Blast this event's buyers" CTA on the event detail screen (pre-fills audience = buyers of that event), and a "Blast all my buyers" CTA on the brand page (pre-fills audience = all buyers of that brand). All three routes land in the same composer. (3) A new permanent "Customers" tab inside each brand lists every person who has ever bought a ticket from that brand, with filter (event, date range, consent state) and a "Blast these N customers" CTA. This is the canonical audience-source UI for the brand-rollup audience. The same data is also surfaced inside an event's detail screen as "Buyers (N)" with its own "Blast this event's buyers" CTA. Same `orders`-derived rows, two read paths, written once. (4) The standalone hub is the FIRST place a brand discovers marketing capability — they see the tab on bottom-nav and know it exists. The contextual entry points are the SECOND, frictionless path for the highest-frequency action ("I'm here, blast these buyers"). Both/and, not either/or. Rationale: pushback considered — pure standalone is discoverable but loses zero-friction event-context blasting; pure embedded is seamless but loses cross-brand rollup, template library, suppression-list management, and a dedicated workspace for recurring organisers. Both/and is the pattern every modern SaaS uses (Shopify Marketing, Linear, Klaviyo). Engineering consequence: `marketing_audiences.query_definition jsonb` declarative shape — event-scoped buyer query and brand-scoped buyer query are two row types with the same shape, with "brand-followers" as a third row type added later when the followers schema lands. Operator + orchestrator agreement 2026-05-12 (mingla-orchestrator skill session). Cross-reference: `Mingla_Artifacts/MINGLA_BUSINESS_MARKETING_HUB_STRATEGY.md` §3.8 (surface placement), ORCH-0815 registration in `Mingla_Artifacts/MASTER_BUG_LIST.md` + `Mingla_Artifacts/PRIORITY_BOARD.md`, `Mingla_Artifacts/github/epics/cycle-b5.md` Phase A note.

> **2026-05-11 - DEC-148 logged - ORCH-0787 close — order refund + cancel production-grade flow is live end-to-end with three architectural rules codified.** Decision: (1) **Edge functions calling SECURITY DEFINER RPCs that read `auth.uid()` MUST use `userClient(req)` (anon key + caller's Authorization header), not `serviceClient()`** — service-role JWTs carry no `sub` claim so `request.jwt.claim.sub` is unset and `auth.uid()` returns NULL inside the SECURITY DEFINER body, collapsing every caller-identity permission gate to closed-fail. Reserve service-role for non-auth-context ops (audit, notifications, lookups). Reference helper: `supabase/functions/_shared/ticketCheckout.ts` `userClient(req: Request)`. Established after F-15/F-16 (ORCH-0787 refund-order + cancel-order returned permission_denied to the legitimate brand owner). (2) **Hermes-safe randomId is mandatory for any Idempotency-Key / per-attempt UUID in `mingla-business`** — bare `crypto.randomUUID()` is a ReferenceError on iOS/Android because Hermes has no global `crypto`. Reference helper: `mingla-business/src/utils/randomId.ts`. Enforced by strict-grep §8.1.10 in `.github/scripts/strict-grep/orch-0787-refund-cancel-flow.mjs`. Established after F-12/F-13. (3) **Test-mode Stripe RAKs for the ticket refund flow require these four permissions: Charges-and-Refunds:Write, Payment-Intents:Read, Connect-Application-Fees:Write, Connect-Transfers:Write** — documented for future RAK rotation, live-mode key creation, and audit. Established after F-RAK. Plus: ORCH-0788 (P1) registered for the missing buyer-notification dispatcher — ORCH-0787 enqueues correct rows in `ticket_order_notifications` with `template_key='buyer_refund_issued'`/`buyer_order_cancelled` but no consumer routes by template_key today. Rationale: operator-witnessed live-fire on 2026-05-11 17:46 UTC produced real $50 USD test-mode refund `re_3TVkS5PjlZyAYA401Z1MzZad` on order `6ad119af-…`; queue row `81fe2a68-…` persists for retroactive replay once ORCH-0788 ships. Evidence: spec `specs/SPEC_ORCH-0787_ORDER_REFUND_AND_CANCEL_PRODUCTION_GRADE.md`, implementation `reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0787_ORDER_REFUND_AND_CANCEL_PRODUCTION_GRADE.md`, QA `reports/QA_ORCH-0787_LIVE_FIRE_REPORT.md` (§10/§11/§12), close note `CLOSE_NOTE_ORCH-0787.md`. Enforcement: 10-assertion strict-grep gate `.github/scripts/strict-grep/orch-0787-refund-cancel-flow.mjs` including §8.1.10 Hermes-safe-randomId; 7 regression specs at `mingla-business/src/utils/__tests__/randomId.test.ts`; refund-order + cancel-order edge functions deployed to v2 with `verify_jwt:true` preserved; `STRIPE_RAK_TICKET_REFUND` Supabase Edge Function secret SET in test mode. > **2026-05-11 - DEC-147 logged - ORCH-0786 close — Mingla Business profile avatar is solved end-to-end across upload, render, persistence, native image cache, and OAuth auth-sync.** Decision: `mingla-business/src/services/creatorAvatarService.ts` is the canonical avatar upload service (read bytes via `expo-file-system`, MIME normalisation, HEAD/Range public-URL verification, path rotation `{userId}.{token}.{ext}`, best-effort orphan delete); `mingla-business/src/services/creatorAccount.ts:ensureCreatorAccount` is seed-only (`ignoreDuplicates: true`, `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`); `mingla-business/app/account/edit-profile.tsx` auto-saves the canonical URL on pick, hides the Save button until `nameDirty`, and the avatar `<Image>` carries an `onError` initials fallback. The Cycle 14 storage RLS predicate `split_part(name, '.', 1) = auth.uid()::text` is preserved byte-equivalent — path rotation introduces a token in the second dot-segment, not the first, so owner gating still resolves to the user id. Evidence: investigation `reports/INVESTIGATION_ORCH-0786_BUSINESS_PROFILE_AVATAR_BLACK_AFTER_CHANGE.md`, spec `specs/SPEC_ORCH-0786_BUSINESS_PROFILE_AVATAR_BLACK_AFTER_CHANGE.md`, implementation `reports/IMPLEMENTATION_ORCH-0786_BUSINESS_PROFILE_AVATAR_BLACK_AFTER_CHANGE.md`, QA `reports/QA_ORCH-0786_BUSINESS_PROFILE_AVATAR_BLACK_AFTER_CHANGE.md`, close note `CLOSE_NOTE_ORCH-0786.md`. > **2026-05-11 - DEC-145 logged - Claude/Codex orchestrator full parity and mandatory pre-merge gate.** Decision: from 2026-05-11 forward, Claude `mingla-orchestrator` and Codex `orchestrator-mingla` are full peers on every pipeline phase including IMPLEMENT and CLOSE. There is no Codex-only or Claude-only phase. Default routing remains (Claude forensics for INVESTIGATE/SPEC/TEST, Codex implementor for IMPLEMENT, either orchestrator for CLOSE) but defaults are preferences, not restrictions. Either orchestrator may write code, deploy edge functions, commit, push, open PRs, run the pre-merge gate, and merge to `main` when operator delegates execution (verbatim cues: "take over", "execute", "do it", "ship it", "close it", "you finish"). In parallel, a mandatory five-condition pre-merge gate is codified: before any `gh pr merge` on any PR (especially `Seth → main`), the orchestrator MUST verify (1) all required GitHub checks GREEN via `gh pr checks --watch`, (2) `mergeable: MERGEABLE` and `mergeStateStatus: CLEAN` (or operator-approved `UNSTABLE`), (3) `reviewDecision: APPROVED` when reviews are required, (4) not `BEHIND` main, and (5) explicit operator confirmation "checks green + conflicts clean — proceeding to merge" in chat. If any condition fails, the merge does not happen — report the blocker, fix root cause, re-verify. Rationale: the operator directed that "Claude can do everything Codex can do" and codified the merge gate after a Seth→main merge was attempted with failing checks and unresolved conflicts. Enforcement: `.claude/skills/mingla-orchestrator/SKILL.md` and `.codex/skills/orchestrator-mingla/SKILL.md` both updated to remove asymmetry and add the pre-merge gate (§"Pre-merge gate (MANDATORY)"); memory files `feedback_claude_codex_full_parity.md` and `feedback_pr_merge_pregate.md` cross-reference. Deploy notes: process/skill change only; no product code, Supabase migration, Edge Function deploy, provider mutation, or EAS OTA.

> **2026-05-10 - DEC-135 logged - META-ORCH-0755 Step 8 worktree discipline (one worktree per ORCH).** Decision: from 2026-05-10 forward, every ORCH that touches product code runs in its own git worktree at `.worktrees/orch-XXXX-<short>/` on branch `orch/XXXX-<short>`, opened at the first INVESTIGATE dispatch from `origin/main` and closed at CLOSE via `git merge --no-ff` + `git worktree remove` + `git branch -d`. The orchestrator (Codex `orchestrator-mingla`, canonical) emits create / sync / close commands; the operator executes them; the Claude `mingla-orchestrator` parity mirror emits the same commands but never executes the close commands itself. Scoped artifacts (investigation/spec/implementation/QA reports for the ORCH) commit inside the worktree on the ORCH branch; global indexes (DECISION_LOG, INVARIANT_REGISTRY, WORLD_MAP, PRIORITY_BOARD, MASTER_BUG_LIST, AGENT_HANDOFFS, COVERAGE_MAP, PRODUCT_SNAPSHOT, ROOT_CAUSE_REGISTER, OPEN_INVESTIGATIONS, WORKTREE_REGISTRY) are written only in main to avoid conflict-merges between concurrent ORCHs. `.claude/skills/` and `.codex/skills/` travel from main into each worktree via symlink, so skill edits propagate immediately to every active worktree. META-ORCHs (process-only work like META-ORCH-0755 itself) run in main with no worktree; hot-fixes still take a worktree; sub-ORCHs each get their own. Phase agents (forensics, implementor, tester) read current index state via `git show main:Mingla_Artifacts/<file>.md` from inside the worktree — never by writing to the worktree's snapshot of those files. Codified inline as a "Worktree Discipline" section in all 8 lifecycle skill `SKILL.md` files; new tracking artifact `Mingla_Artifacts/WORKTREE_REGISTRY.md` created (committable, owned by Codex `orchestrator-mingla`); ratified as I-PROPOSED-AC ONE_WORKTREE_PER_ORCH. Rationale: the prior default (everyone working in main on whatever branch is checked out) caused mid-cycle bleed between ORCHs, branch-switch state loss, and constant index-file merge conflicts between concurrent CLOSE-outs. Per-ORCH worktrees give clean isolation; index-files-in-main-only gives clean concurrency. Full strategy with worktree-lifecycle commands, alternatives considered (single tree, per-cycle, per-domain), and edge cases at `Mingla_Artifacts/WORKTREE_STRATEGY.md`. Operator directive: "best worktree strategy to implement across all claude and codex skills that creates sync and coordination across skills, but also makes sure that one working tree is used for related work until it closes."

D-CLOSE-4 Gemini auto-retry-once: **ratified production** — Cary smoke had 3/50 rows fail with `MALFORMED_FUNCTION_CALL`, all 3 succeeded on retry; per-row reliability went from ~94-96% to ~99.9% with negligible cost. D-CLOSE-5 stratified-random sampling: **ratified production** — top half by review_count guarantees signal-rich anchors get exercised; Fisher-Yates fill provides representative tail-of-distribution coverage; chosen over pure random (would skew toward sparse-data rows) and over top-N-by-rating (would skew toward famous places already covered). D-CLOSE-6 admin UI form discipline: **city picker + sample-size picker BOTH required** (Run button disabled until both selected) — chosen over default-fallback (would silently run wrong city if operator forgets to pick) and over numeric sample-size input (constrained dropdown 50/100/200/500 prevents typos). Up to 16 keys per place; only signals where this place was evaluated. **Zero new production tables.** **Cut 3 (operator-driven)** — `signal_anchors` table served only calibration purpose (proved Claude works on 32-anchor run). | DEC-088 | 2026-05-02 | **ORCH-0708 — Score `cap` raised 200 → 1000 on 7 dining/family signals (fine_dining, brunch, romantic, lively, casual_food, drinks, icebreakers); other 9 signals stay at cap=200. Raw uncapped scores recomputed from `contributions` JSONB reach 459 (casual_food), 456 (drinks), 375 (brunch), 361 (icebreakers) — meaning a flagship venue raw=400 and a mediocre venue raw=200 both currently clamp to the same 200. Other 9 signals (movies, theatre, scenic, picnic_friendly, creative_arts, play, nature, flowers, groceries) have cap-compression rates ≤0.4% — no current need to raise. clamp_min stays 0 across all signals; `min_rating` + `min_reviews` hard gates unchanged. filter_min thresholds at the serving layer (typically 80–120) STAY ABSOLUTE in this dispatch — re-evaluate per signal after first re-score in Raleigh/Cary/Durham. | Operator approved photo-aesthetic-scoring integration (Wave 2 Phase 1). Photo-derived field weights need a budget that today's 200-cap suffocates — adding 7-12 photo weights inside a 200 budget that already gives 60 to popularity + 50 to text patterns would force every photo weight to ±5 to ±10, too small to materially shift ranking. Cap raise unlocks meaningful photo contribution. | (A) Keep cap=200, fit photo weights inside existing budget — rejected: every photo weight forced to ±5 to ±10 magnitude; won't move ranking. (B) Move to per-city percentile serving (Option C) — architecturally correct but requires touching every consumer of `place_scores` (5 functions + 1 RPC) + nightly percentile compute job + re-tuning across 16 signals. (C) Raise cap to 500 — rejected: split-the-baby; 1000 buys clean 5x headroom for the casual_food raw=459 ceiling without tight-fit anxiety. | filter_min thresholds become a smaller fraction of the cap (filter_min: 120 / cap: 1000 = 12% vs 60% before). Need re-evaluation per signal after first re-score in Raleigh/Cary/Durham — operator verifies "is filter_min still excluding the right things?" If too many places clear, bump threshold (e.g., 120 → 200 for fine_dining). Acceptable cost: this is a one-time tuning pass, not a recurring expense. The 9 untouched signals still respect cap=200 — if their photo weights ever land (currently out of scope), they'll need their own cap raises. | Permanent for the 7 in-scope signals' v_next configs. Exit signals: (a) ORCH-0709 ships per-city percentile — at that point absolute filter_min becomes obsolete on the 7 signals and the cap is decoupled from the serving threshold entirely. (b) Future signals receive photo weights → revisit cap raise on a case-by-case basis (no blanket commitment beyond the 7 named here). (c) If after Raleigh+Cary+Durham smoke the cap=1000 is materially under-utilized (max raw still <300 post-photo for any signal), consider lowering it to 500 in v_next+1 to avoid score-distribution fatigue in admin UIs. |

| DEC-079 | 2026-04-29 | **ORCH-BIZ-KIT-CLOSURE-CARVEOUT — Cycle 0a kit (24 primitives) is "closed for new primitives or new behaviours" but NOT closed for retiring transitional behaviours via additive props.** Primitives that ship with `// [TRANSITIONAL]` markers in their own header comments (e.g., TopBar's Cycle 0a Toast on brand-chip-tap saying "lands in Cycle 2") MAY be extended in subsequent cycles with **additive props** that retire the transitional behaviour. Example: TopBar gains `onBrandTap?: () => void` in Cycle 1 — when undefined, Toast still fires (backward compatible); when defined, Toast is suppressed and the caller's handler runs. NEW behaviours not previously anticipated by the primitive's header comments still hard-stop and require orchestrator escalation. Implementor dispatches that touch primitives must explicitly cite this DEC plus the line of the primitive's `// [TRANSITIONAL]` marker that anchors the carve-out. | Founder approved 2026-04-29 after Cycle 1 implementor surfaced contradiction: AC#4 of Cycle 1 dispatch required TopBar's brand-chip to open BrandSwitcherSheet, but kit-closure rule from Cycle 1 dispatch §3.6 forbade extending primitives. The TopBar header comment already marked the Toast as Cycle 0a-transitional ("lands in Cycle 2"), so the carve-out is structurally implicit but needed explicit ratification. | (A) Kit fully closed — every transitional behaviour requires a brand-new primitive in subsequent cycles. Rejected: explodes primitive count and creates duplicate components for the same logical control. (B) Kit fully open — any cycle freely modifies primitives. Rejected: defeats kit-closure's scope-creep guard. (C) Kit hard-replaceable — Cycle 1+ may rewrite Cycle 0a primitives. Rejected: breaks existing call sites and re-opens settled foundation. | Implementors must distinguish "additive prop to retire transitional" (allowed) from "behaviour change to existing consumer" (hard-stop). The line is bright: if the primitive's existing call sites continue to render identical UX without code changes, the prop is additive; if they don't, it's a behaviour change. Slight discipline tax — every additive prop must be justified against a `// [TRANSITIONAL]` anchor in the primitive's source. | Permanent process rule. The kit's "closed for new primitives" guard remains. The carve-out applies ONLY to retiring transitionals via additive props, ONLY when the primitive's own source explicitly anticipated future wiring. |

| DEC-044 | 2026-04-22 | **ORCH-0640 — NORTH STAR locked: the ONE card-generation pipeline is the 3-gate signal path. A card is ELIGIBLE if and only if G1 ∧ G2 ∧ G3 all hold. Every serving RPC in the repo must enforce all three. I need you to pin this down as the goal."* Live probes 2026-04-22 confirm: 9,989 places pass all 3 gates currently (out of 10,010 scored × 12,356 Bouncer-approved × 17,477 photo-complete); every one of 15 signals has full coverage (100% of scored places pass photo+Bouncer gates per signal). **Photo-gate leak discovered:** 9 places are `is_servable=true` with no real photos; `query_servable_places_by_signal` LIVE body MISSING gate G3 (photo check) — see §Finding-G3 below. | Alternative gate orderings (photo-first, score-first, bouncer-first) — rejected: the user's ordering is the operational priority. Dual-secret signature verification: tries `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` first; on signature failure retries with `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET_PREVIOUS` if set (D-V3-9 rotation pattern). ## DEC-162 — GlassSessionSwitcher + CollaborationSessions DECOMMISSIONED per META-ORCH-0929 (2026-05-23)

**Decision:** Delete `app-mobile/src/components/GlassSessionSwitcher.tsx` (654 lines) and `app-mobile/src/components/CollaborationSessions.tsx` (~1915 lines) entirely. No replacement at parity — the responsibilities split across 5 new focused components per META-ORCH-0929 SPEC §2.1.

**Rationale:** Operator directive 2026-05-23 ("one clean pass — no transitional duplicate code, no dead instances"). The two legacy components tangled session switching + session creation + invite-pill rendering + paywall gating + per-session view modal into ~2,569 lines on the Home surface. The redesign separates these concerns: Home = solo deck (no session UI), Friends `+` chooser = session creation entry, MessageInterface header = per-chat CollabDeckSheet launcher, chat list = pending-invite rows. Net code reduction −1101 lines while ADDING new functionality (5 components + 17 i18n keys + ORCH-0926 fold).

**Citation:** META-ORCH-0929 CLOSE banner in `WORLD_MAP.md` 2026-05-23. SPEC §2.1 deletion list. QA report `Mingla_Artifacts/reports/QA_META-ORCH-0929_*.md` verifies absence via static guards.
